<!-- Client Side Interactive Script for Demo Puzzle -->
<script is:inline>
  const optionButtons = document.querySelectorAll('.option-btn');
  const feedbackBox = document.getElementById('demo-feedback');

  optionButtons.forEach((btn) => {
    btn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      const isCorrect = btn.getAttribute('data-correct') === 'true';
      const optionLabel = btn.getAttribute('data-option');

      optionButtons.forEach((b) => {
        b.classList.remove(
          'border-emerald-500', 'bg-emerald-50/70', 'dark:bg-emerald-950/40',
          'border-red-500', 'bg-red-50/70', 'dark:bg-red-950/40'
        );
      });

      if (isCorrect) {
        btn.classList.add('border-emerald-500', 'bg-emerald-50/70', 'dark:bg-emerald-950/40');
        if (feedbackBox) {
          feedbackBox.innerHTML = `
            <div class="text-sm sm:text-base font-bold text-emerald-600 dark:text-emerald-400 mb-1">Correct! Option ${optionLabel} is the exact solution.</div>
            <div class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300">Rule: In each horizontal row, shape count increases progressively (1 to 2 to 3) while keeping geometry and color constant. Third row requires 3 purple triangles.</div>
          `;
          feedbackBox.className = "mt-5 p-4 sm:p-5 rounded-2xl bg-emerald-50/60 dark:bg-emerald-950/30 border border-emerald-300 dark:border-emerald-800 text-center transition-all";
        }
      } else {
        btn.classList.add('border-red-500', 'bg-red-50/70', 'dark:bg-red-950/40');
        if (feedbackBox) {
          feedbackBox.innerHTML = `
            <div class="text-sm sm:text-base font-bold text-red-600 dark:text-red-400 mb-1">Option ${optionLabel} is not correct.</div>
            <div class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300">Hint: Count the number of shapes in each row from left to right. Notice the pattern progression.</div>
          `;
          feedbackBox.className = "mt-5 p-4 sm:p-5 rounded-2xl bg-red-50/60 dark:bg-red-950/30 border border-red-300 dark:border-red-800 text-center transition-all";
        }
      }
    });
  });
</script>\n

    <!-- Section 1: Executive Summary & Theoretical Foundations (CHC) -->
    <section id="section-1-chc" class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs space-y-6">
      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Section 01
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Executive Summary & Theoretical Foundations (CHC Framework)
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Contemporary psychometric science grounds human cognitive assessment in the Cattell-Horn-Carroll (CHC) three-stratum theory of cognitive abilities. Under CHC theory, general cognitive ability (Stratum III g) is composed of distinct broad cognitive domains (Stratum II), each supported by specialized narrow operational abilities (Stratum I).
        </p>
      </div>

      <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
        FreeIQExam isolates four core Stratum II broad domains that demonstrate the strongest empirical loadings on general intelligence while minimizing cultural and linguistic bias:
      </p>

      <!-- 4 Core Domains Grid -->
      <div class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-5">
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center gap-2 mb-2">
            <span class="w-2.5 h-2.5 rounded-full bg-blue-600"></span>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              1. Fluid Reasoning (Gf)
            </h3>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-3">
            The capacity to extract abstract rules, solve novel non-verbal problems, and apply logical operations in unfamiliar environments. Measured via inductive 3x3 matrix reasoning, topological XOR transformations, and figural series completions.
          </p>
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-semibold">
            Master Bank: 27 Calibrated Items
          </span>
        </div>

        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center gap-2 mb-2">
            <span class="w-2.5 h-2.5 rounded-full bg-indigo-600"></span>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              2. Visual-Spatial Processing (Gv)
            </h3>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-3">
            The ability to perceive, generate, store, retrieve, and mentally rotate visual patterns and 3D objects in mental space. Measured via rigid 3D cube rotations, 2D-to-3D flat folding net syntheses, and orthographic projections.
          </p>
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 font-semibold">
            Master Bank: 25 Calibrated Items
          </span>
        </div>

        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center gap-2 mb-2">
            <span class="w-2.5 h-2.5 rounded-full bg-emerald-600"></span>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              3. Quantitative Reasoning (Gq)
            </h3>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-3">
            The ability to comprehend quantitative properties, identify nested mathematical relationships, and solve relational numerical structures. Measured via interleaved inductive progressions and relational algebraic rate problems.
          </p>
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-emerald-600 dark:text-emerald-400 font-semibold">
            Master Bank: 24 Calibrated Items
          </span>
        </div>

        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center gap-2 mb-2">
            <span class="w-2.5 h-2.5 rounded-full bg-purple-600"></span>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              4. Verbal Comprehension (Gc)
            </h3>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-3">
            The breadth and depth of knowledge acquired within linguistic systems, abstract relational lexical analogies, semantic hierarchies, and formal Aristotelian categorical syllogistic logic.
          </p>
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 font-semibold">
            Master Bank: 24 Calibrated Items
          </span>

    <!-- Section 2: Mathematical Formulation of 2PL IRT Model -->
    <section id="section-2-2pl" class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs space-y-6">
      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Section 02
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Mathematical Formulation of the 2-Parameter Logistic (2PL) Model
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Classical Test Theory (CTT) treats every test item identically, scoring ability as an unweighted sum of correct responses. This creates severe sample dependency: raw scores cannot differentiate whether an examinee succeeded on trivial or exceptionally arduous items. FreeIQExam overcomes this limitation by implementing the 2-Parameter Logistic (2PL) Item Response Theory model.
        </p>
      </div>

      <!-- Formula Block -->
      <div class="p-5 sm:p-6 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 space-y-3">
        <div class="text-xs font-mono text-zinc-400 uppercase tracking-wider">The Standard 2PL IRT Equation</div>
        <div class="font-mono text-base sm:text-lg text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-bold overflow-x-auto py-1">
          P_i(&theta;) = 1 / [ 1 + exp( -1.702 &times; a_i &times; (&theta; - b_i) ) ]
        </div>
        <div class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed space-y-1.5 pt-2">
          <p>Where:</p>
          <ul class="list-disc list-inside space-y-1 pl-1">
            <li><strong>&theta; (Theta):</strong> The latent cognitive trait of the examinee, distributed along the standard normal continuum N(0, 1).</li>
            <li><strong>a_i (Discrimination):</strong> The slope of the Item Characteristic Curve (ICC) at the inflection point, characterizing how sharply the item differentiates between examinees above versus below the threshold.</li>
            <li><strong>b_i (Difficulty):</strong> The latent ability level at which the probability of a correct response is exactly 50% (P_i(&theta;) = 0.50).</li>
            <li><strong>D = 1.702:</strong> The normal ogive scaling constant ensuring mathematical concordance between the logistic metric and the cumulative normal distribution function &Phi;(z).</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
      </div>

      <!-- Interactive / Responsive SVG Item Characteristic Curves (ICC) -->
      <div>
        <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Item Characteristic Curves (ICC) &amp; Discrimination Dynamics
        </h3>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
          The diagram below visualizes the mathematical Item Characteristic Curves for three calibrated benchmark items: an Easy item (b = -1.5, a = 1.2), a Medium item (b = 0.0, a = 1.4), and a Hard item (b = +1.5, a = 1.7).
        </p>

        <div class="p-4 sm:p-6 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 overflow-x-auto">
          <svg viewBox="0 0 640 260" class="w-full min-w-[560px] h-auto select-none font-mono text-xs" fill="none" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">

    </section>

    <!-- Section 3: Bayesian Expected A Posteriori (EAP) Estimation Engine -->
    <section id="section-3-eap" class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs space-y-6">
      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Section 03
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Bayesian Expected A Posteriori (EAP) Estimation Engine
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          While Maximum Likelihood Estimation (MLE) is widely used in long academic exams, it suffers from a catastrophic mathematical failure mode on short assessments: when an examinee scores 0 out of 24 (or 24 out of 24), the likelihood function is strictly monotonic, causing MLE to diverge to negative or positive infinity.
        </p>
      </div>

      <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
        FreeIQExam solves this by implementing Bayesian Expected A Posteriori (EAP) estimation with a standardized Gaussian informative prior g(&theta;) ~ N(0, 1). This guarantees finite, robust, and regularized ability estimates regardless of response vector extremeness.
      </p>

      <!-- Numerical Quadrature Equation -->
      <div class="p-5 sm:p-6 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 space-y-3">
        <div class="text-xs font-mono text-zinc-400 uppercase tracking-wider">Gauss-Hermite Numerical Quadrature (81 Nodes)</div>
        <div class="font-mono text-sm sm:text-base text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-bold overflow-x-auto py-1">
          &theta;_hat_EAP = [ &Sigma;<sub>k=1..Q</sub> [ &theta;_k &times; L(&theta;_k | u) &times; W(&theta;_k) ] / &Sigma;<sub>k=1..Q</sub> [ L(&theta;_k | u) &times; W(&theta;_k) ]
        </div>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          The engine computes the integral across Q = 81 equidistant evaluation nodes spanning &theta;_k &isin; [-4.0, +4.0] with node step size &Delta;&theta; = 0.10. For any vector of binary item responses u = (u_1, u_2, ..., u_n), the response pattern likelihood is:
        </p>
        <div class="font-mono text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-200 py-1">
          L(&theta;_k | u) = &prod;<sub>i=1..n</sub> [ P_i(&theta;_k)<sup>u_i</sup> &times; (1 - P_i(&theta;_k))<sup>1 - u_i</sup> ]
        </div>
        <p class="text-xs text-zinc-500 font-mono">
          Where prior weighting W(&theta;_k) = (1 / &radic;(2&pi;)) &times; exp(-&theta;_k^2 / 2) &times; &Delta;&theta;.
        </p>
      </div>

      <!-- Posterior Variance & 95% Credible Interval -->
      <div class="p-5 sm:p-6 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 space-y-3">
        <div class="text-xs font-mono text-zinc-400 uppercase tracking-wider">Conditional SEM & 95% Credible Interval</div>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Unlike Classical Test Theory, which assumes an invariant standard error for all candidates, IRT models conditional measurement error across the trait continuum. The posterior variance is computed directly from the second moment of the posterior distribution:
        </p>
        <div class="font-mono text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-200 py-1">
          Var(&theta; | u) = &Sigma;<sub>k=1..Q</sub> [ (&theta;_k - &theta;_hat_EAP)<sup>2</sup> &times; L(&theta;_k | u) &times; W(&theta;_k) ] / &Sigma;<sub>k=1..Q</sub> [ L(&theta;_k | u) &times; W(&theta;_k) ]
        </div>
        <div class="font-mono text-xs sm:text-sm text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 font-semibold py-1">
          SEM(&theta;) = &radic;Var(&theta; | u)
        </div>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          The 95% Posterior Credible Interval reported on candidate score certificates is formally derived as:
        </p>
        <div class="font-mono text-xs sm:text-sm text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-bold py-1">
          Interval<sub>95%</sub> = [ IQ - 1.96 &times; 15 &times; SEM(&theta;), &nbsp; IQ + 1.96 &times; 15 &times; SEM(&theta;) ]
        </div>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Section 4: Scale Standardization vs. Population Norming -->
    <section id="section-4-standardization" class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs space-y-6">
      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Section 04
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Scale Standardization vs. Population Norming
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          A fundamental scientific requirement under the Standards for Educational and Psychological Testing (AERA, APA, NCME, 2014) is absolute transparency regarding how raw ability metrics are translated into standardized scores.
        </p>
      </div>

      <!-- Linear Transformation Formula -->
      <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
        <span class="text-xs font-mono text-zinc-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-1">Standardized Linear Transformation</span>
        <div class="font-mono text-base sm:text-lg font-bold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 mb-2">
          Standardized Score = round( 100 + 15 &times; &theta;_hat_EAP )
        </div>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Percentiles are calculated via the standard normal cumulative distribution function &Phi;(z):
        </p>
        <div class="font-mono text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-200 mt-1">
          Percentile = &Phi;( (Standardized Score - 100) / 15 ) &times; 100%
        </div>
      </div>

      <!-- Critical Distinction Card -->
      <div class="p-5 sm:p-6 rounded-2xl bg-amber-50/70 dark:bg-amber-950/30 border border-amber-200 dark:border-amber-900 text-xs sm:text-sm text-amber-950 dark:text-amber-200 leading-relaxed space-y-3">
        <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base uppercase tracking-wider text-amber-900 dark:text-amber-300">
          Scientific Clarification: Scale Metric vs. Clinical Population Norms
        </h3>
        <p>
          1. <strong>Scale Transformation:</strong> FreeIQExam projects latent trait theta (&theta;) onto the classical Wechsler/Stanford-Binet metric (Mean = 100, SD = 15). This provides a universally recognized reference scale for relative cognitive performance.
        </p>
        <p>
          2. <strong>Empirical Population Norms:</strong> Establishing formal demographic population norms requires stratified sampling across thousands of clinical subjects segmented by age, geographic region, and educational attainment. FreeIQExam does not claim clinical diagnosis; our scores reflect mathematically rigorous 2PL IRT estimates anchored to verified open-science benchmarks.
        </p>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Section 5: Sub-Domain Reporting Rationale -->
    <section id="section-5-subdomains" class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs space-y-6">
      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Section 05
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Sub-Domain Reporting Rationale
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          In the 24-item Full Assessment, examinees complete 6 items per cognitive domain (Fluid, Spatial, Quantitative, Verbal). A common pitfall in commercial online testing is reporting continuous IQ sub-scores for short sub-tests (e.g. claiming "Spatial IQ = 138" based on 6 items).
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 space-y-3">
        <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
          The Problem of Wide Conditional Standard Errors on Short Vectors
        </h3>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          On a 6-item domain vector, the conditional standard error of measurement is wide (SEM &approx; 0.45 to 0.65 &theta;, equivalent to &plusmn;7 to &plusmn;10 IQ points). Producing continuous pseudo-precise sub-scores produces spurious precision that misleads candidates and violates psychometric reporting standards.
        </p>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          FreeIQExam adopts a conservative, scientifically defensible solution:
        </p>
        <ul class="list-disc list-inside text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300 space-y-1 pl-1">
          <li><strong>Domain Accuracy Ratios:</strong> We report raw performance (e.g., "5 / 6 Correct, 83%").</li>
          <li><strong>Qualitative Performance Tiers:</strong> Performance is mapped into descriptive bands:
            <span class="font-mono text-xs font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400">Strength Area (&ge;83%)</span>,
            <span class="font-mono text-xs font-semibold text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400">High Average (67-82%)</span>,
            <span class="font-mono text-xs font-semibold text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300">Average Range (50-66%)</span>, and
            <span class="font-mono text-xs font-semibold text-amber-600 dark:text-amber-400">Developing Area (&lt;50%)</span>.
          </li>
          <li><strong>Dedicated 20-Item Tests:</strong> For candidates seeking continuous psychometric sub-scale precision, FreeIQExam provides four dedicated 20-item single-domain test batteries where measurement error is constrained to acceptable limits.</li>
        </ul>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Section 6: Transparent Calibration & Open-Science Roadmap -->
    <section id="section-6-roadmap" class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs space-y-6">
      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Section 06
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Transparent Calibration & Open-Science Validation Roadmap
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Open science requires candid disclosure of item provenance and a structured recalibration roadmap. FreeIQExam operates on a two-phase developmental validation pipeline:
        </p>
      </div>

      <!-- Roadmap Milestones -->
      <div class="space-y-4">
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-2 mb-2">
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              Phase 0: Research-Anchored Development Seeds (Current)
            </h3>
            <span class="px-2.5 py-0.5 rounded-full text-xs font-mono font-bold bg-blue-100 text-blue-800 dark:bg-blue-950 dark:text-blue-300">Active</span>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
            The difficulty parameters (b_i &isin; [-2.20, +2.60]) and discrimination parameters (a_i &isin; [0.95, 2.00]) embedded in the 100-item bank represent research-anchored seeds synthesized from peer-reviewed psychometric benchmarks in the International Cognitive Ability Resource (Condon &amp; Revelle, 2014; Dworak et al., 2021) and Sandia National Laboratories matrix banks (Matzen et al., 2010).
          </p>
        </div>

        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-2 mb-2">
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              Phase 1 Milestone (Target: N = 500 Completed Sessions)
            </h3>
            <span class="px-2.5 py-0.5 rounded-full text-xs font-mono font-bold bg-zinc-200 text-zinc-700 dark:bg-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-400">Roadmap</span>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
            Upon reaching 500 complete test administrations, Classical Test Theory (CTT) item difficulty indices (p-values) and corrected point-biserial discrimination coefficients (r_pb) will be computed. Distractor analysis will verify that incorrect alternatives draw proportionally from lower-ability respondents without ambiguity.
          </p>
        </div>

        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-2 mb-2">
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              Phase 2 Milestone (Target: N = 1,000 Completed Sessions)
            </h3>
            <span class="px-2.5 py-0.5 rounded-full text-xs font-mono font-bold bg-zinc-200 text-zinc-700 dark:bg-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-400">Roadmap</span>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
            Full item recalibration via Marginal Maximum Likelihood (MML) estimation using the Expectation-Maximization (EM) algorithm implemented via the standard psychometric package mirt (Bock &amp; Aitkin, 1981). Item fit will be formally audited using Orlando-Thissen S-&chi;^2 statistics.
          </p>
        </div>

        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-800/60 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700/80">
          <div class="flex items-center justify-between gap-2 mb-2">
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
              Fairness &amp; Differential Item Functioning (DIF) Screening
            </h3>
            <span class="px-2.5 py-0.5 rounded-full text-xs font-mono font-bold bg-zinc-200 text-zinc-700 dark:bg-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-400">Continuous</span>
          </div>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
            To guard against cultural, linguistic, or subgroup bias, items undergo continuous Differential Item Functioning (DIF) screening via the Mantel-Haenszel procedure and logistic regression. Items displaying significant uniform or non-uniform DIF are retired or revised.
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </section>

      <div>
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">

    <!-- Educational Guide Section -->
    <section class="space-y-8">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Cognitive Problem-Solving Masterclass
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
          Master the formal principles that cognitive scientists and psychometricians use to construct high-difficulty test items.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-3 gap-6">
        <!-- 1. Carpenter's Matrix Rules -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <div class="w-10 h-10 rounded-2xl bg-blue-100 dark:bg-blue-950 text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-mono font-bold flex items-center justify-center mb-4">
            1
          </div>
          <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
            Carpenter's 5 Matrix Rules
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed mb-4">
            In their 1990 Cognitive Psychology paper, Carpenter, Just, and Shell identified 5 universal transformation rules across matrix puzzles:
          </p>
          <ul class="space-y-1.5 text-xs text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300">
            <li>&bull; <strong>Constant in row:</strong> Shape remains invariant horizontally.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>Quantitative progression:</strong> Dot count increases (+1, +2).</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>Distribution of 3 values:</strong> Latin square permutation.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>Distribution of 2 values:</strong> XOR cancellation of overlapping segments.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>Movement &amp; rotation:</strong> Clockwise 45 or 90 degree step progression.</li>
          </ul>
        </div>

        <!-- 2. Mentally Unrolling 3D Cube Nets -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <div class="w-10 h-10 rounded-2xl bg-indigo-100 dark:bg-indigo-950 text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 font-mono font-bold flex items-center justify-center mb-4">
            2
          </div>
          <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
            Mentally Unrolling 3D Cube Nets
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed mb-4">
            To quickly eliminate incorrect 3D cube projections without getting disoriented:
          </p>
          <ul class="space-y-1.5 text-xs text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300">
            <li>&bull; <strong>Opposite Face Invariance:</strong> Faces separated by one square in the net will be opposite in the 3D cube. If you see both in an option, eliminate it instantly.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>T-Junction Vertex Rule:</strong> The corners meeting at a 3-way seam will touch in the assembled solid.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>Chirality &amp; Handedness:</strong> Check whether arrows or diagonal lines spiral clockwise or counter-clockwise relative to their neighbor.</li>
          </ul>
        </div>

        <!-- 3. Diagramming Formal Syllogisms -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <div class="w-10 h-10 rounded-2xl bg-purple-100 dark:bg-purple-950 text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 font-mono font-bold flex items-center justify-center mb-4">
            3
          </div>
          <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
            Diagramming Formal Syllogisms
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed mb-4">
            Formal Aristotelian logic requires deriving conclusions with 100% deductive necessity:
          </p>
          <ul class="space-y-1.5 text-xs text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300">
            <li>&bull; <strong>Universal Affirmative:</strong> "All A are B" means the set of A is completely enclosed within set B.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>Particular Affirmative:</strong> "Some A are B" means the intersection of set A and B is non-empty.</li>
            <li>&bull; <strong>The Negative Premise Rule:</strong> If one premise is negative, a valid conclusion must also be negative. Two negative premises yield no valid syllogistic deduction.</li>
          </ul>
        </div>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Frequently Asked Questions Section -->
    <section class="space-y-6 pt-6 border-t border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Frequently Asked Questions: Practice &amp; Training
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
          Everything you need to know about cognitive drills, transformation rules, and scoring transfer.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="space-y-3">
        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is the difference between timed tests and untimed practice drills?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Timed assessments simulate standardized test environments under 15-minute limits to benchmark performance speed. Untimed practice drills provide immediate solution explanations after each question so you can study the underlying transformation rules at your own pace.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What are Carpenter's 5 rules for matrix reasoning?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Carpenter, Just, and Shell (1990) identified 5 primary transformation algorithms in progressive matrices: constant in a row, quantitative progression, distribution of three values, distribution of two values (XOR cancellation), and rotational movement.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Can practicing cognitive drills improve my official IQ score?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Practice improves test familiarity, reduces anxiety, and builds pattern-recognition schemas for transformation rules. While innate fluid ability has high genetic heritability, eliminating procedural mistakes and distractor traps often yields a 5 to 10 point gain in measured test performance.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Do high-IQ societies test these same question types?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Yes. High-IQ societies like Mensa, Intertel, and Triple Nine utilize standardized batteries (such as the WAIS-IV, Stanford-Binet, Cattell Culture Fair, and Raven's APM) that heavily feature matrix induction, mental rotation, numerical series, and verbal analogies.
          </p>
        </details>
      </div>
    </section>

      <div class="overflow-x-auto rounded-3xl border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs">

    <!-- Normal Distribution & Bell Curve Mechanics -->
    <section class="space-y-6">
      <h2 class="font-heading text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-bold text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
        The Normal Distribution &amp; Bell Curve Mechanics
      </h2>
      <div class="prose dark:prose-invert max-w-none text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 space-y-4 leading-relaxed">
        <p>
          All modern IQ assessments are <strong>deviation IQ tests</strong>. Pioneered by David Wechsler in 1939 to replace William Stern's outdated ratio concept (Mental Age divided by Chronological Age multiplied by 100), deviation IQ anchors performance directly to a Gaussian normal distribution.
        </p>
        <p>
          In a standardized deviation IQ model:
        </p>
        <ul class="space-y-2 list-disc pl-5">
          <li><strong>The Mean (&mu;) is fixed at 100:</strong> The median score of any representative age-matched population cohort is defined as 100.</li>
          <li><strong>The Standard Deviation (&sigma;) is set to 15:</strong> One standard deviation represents 15 IQ points on the Wechsler and Stanford-Binet 5 scales.</li>
          <li><strong>The 68-95-99.7 Rule:</strong> Exactly 68.26% of human scores fall within &plusmn;1 standard deviation (IQ 85 to 115). Exactly 95.44% fall within &plusmn;2 standard deviations (IQ 70 to 130). Exactly 99.73% fall within &plusmn;3 standard deviations (IQ 55 to 145).</li>
        </ul>
      </div>

      <!-- Key Percentile Breakdown Grid -->
      <div class="grid grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-3 gap-4 pt-2">
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-semibold uppercase">Within &plusmn;1 SD</span>
          <p class="font-heading font-extrabold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white my-1">68.26% of People</p>
          <p class="text-xs text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">Scores between 85 and 115. Encompasses Low Average, Average, and High Average.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 font-semibold uppercase">Within &plusmn;2 SD</span>
          <p class="font-heading font-extrabold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white my-1">95.44% of People</p>
          <p class="text-xs text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">Scores between 70 and 130. Only 2.28% score higher, and only 2.28% score lower.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <span class="text-xs font-mono text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 font-semibold uppercase">Within &plusmn;3 SD</span>
          <p class="font-heading font-extrabold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white my-1">99.73% of People</p>
          <p class="text-xs text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">Scores between 55 and 145. Scores above 145 occur in roughly 1 out of 740 individuals.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- The Flynn Effect Section -->
    <section class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50/80 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/80 dark:border-zinc-800/80 shadow-xs space-y-4">
      <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block">
        Longitudinal Psychometrics
      </span>
      <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
        The Flynn Effect &amp; Generational Norm Recalibration
      </h2>
      <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
        Discovered by political scientist James R. Flynn, the <strong>Flynn Effect</strong> observes that raw scores on intelligence tests increased at an average rate of approximately 3 IQ points per decade throughout the 20th century across industrialized nations.
      </p>
      <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
        Because of this generational upward shift in raw problem-solving speed and visual abstraction, an individual who scored 100 on an exam normed in 1950 would score noticeably lower on a test calibrated to 2026 norms. To prevent artificial inflation of cognitive classifications, psychometric publishers restandardize their normative test samples every 10 to 15 years.
      </p>
    </section>

    <!-- Assessment Dual Callout CTA -->

      </p>

    <section class="space-y-6">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl sm:text-3xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Frequently Asked Questions: IQ Classification
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
          Clear answers to common questions about intelligence scales, scoring bands, and clinical interpretations.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="space-y-3">
        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is considered a normal or average IQ score?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            On all modern standardized psychometric batteries (including the WAIS-IV and Stanford-Binet 5), an IQ score between 90 and 109 is classified as Average. Exactly 50 percent of the general population falls between scores of 90 and 110.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What IQ score is classified as genius or gifted?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Clinically, a score of 130 or higher (two standard deviations above the population mean of 100 on an SD 15 scale) is classified as Very Superior or Gifted, placing an individual in the top 2.2 percent of the population. High-IQ societies like Mensa require a qualifying threshold of 130 or higher.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is the difference between WAIS-IV and Stanford-Binet classifications?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            The Wechsler Adult Intelligence Scale (WAIS-IV) uses a standard deviation of 15 points, labeling scores of 130 and above as Very Superior. Stanford-Binet 5 uses a standard deviation of 15 (historical SB scales used SD 16) and labels scores of 130 to 144 as Gifted or Very Advanced.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is the Flynn Effect?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            The Flynn effect describes the documented secular rise in raw intelligence test scores across successive generations throughout the 20th century (approximately 3 IQ points per decade). To maintain an accurate mean of 100, psychometric publishers recalibrate test norms every 10 to 15 years.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Can an IQ classification change over a person's lifetime?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            While relative adult IQ shows high test-retest reliability (correlations between 0.80 and 0.90), individual scores can fluctuate due to testing conditions, measurement error (SEM of plus-or-minus 3 to 5 points), health, stress, and practice effects.
          </p>
        </details>
      </div>
    </section>

<script>
  function erf(x: number): number {
    const sign = x >= 0 ? 1 : -1;
    const absX = Math.abs(x);
    const a1 = 0.254829592;
    const a2 = -0.284496736;
    const a3 = 1.421413741;
    const a4 = -1.453152027;
    const a5 = 1.061405429;
    const p = 0.3275911;
    const t = 1.0 / (1.0 + p * absX);
    const y = 1.0 - (((((a5 * t + a4) * t + a3) * t + a2) * t + a1) * t) * Math.exp(-absX * absX);
    return sign * y;
  }

  function normalCdf(z: number): number {
    return 0.5 * (1.0 + erf(z / Math.SQRT2));
  }

  document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
    const rangeEl = document.getElementById('iq-calc-range') as HTMLInputElement;
    const inputEl = document.getElementById('iq-calc-input') as HTMLInputElement;
    const tierEl = document.getElementById('widget-tier');
    const pctEl = document.getElementById('widget-pct');
    const rarityEl = document.getElementById('widget-rarity');
    const zscoreEl = document.getElementById('widget-zscore');

    function updateWidget(iq: number) {
      const z = (iq - 100) / 15;
      const pct = Math.min(99.99, Math.max(0.01, normalCdf(z) * 100));

      let tier = "Average";
      if (iq >= 130) tier = "Very Superior";
      else if (iq >= 120) tier = "Superior";
      else if (iq >= 110) tier = "High Average";
      else if (iq >= 90)  tier = "Average";
      else if (iq >= 80)  tier = "Low Average";
      else if (iq >= 70)  tier = "Borderline";
      else tier = "Extremely Low";

      if (tierEl) tierEl.textContent = tier;
      if (pctEl) pctEl.textContent = `${pct.toFixed(1)}th`;
      if (zscoreEl) zscoreEl.textContent = `${z >= 0 ? '+' : ''}${z.toFixed(2)} σ`;

      if (rarityEl) {
        if (pct >= 50) {
          const rem = 100 - pct;
          const ratio = rem > 0.05 ? Math.round(100 / rem) : 1000;
          rarityEl.textContent = `1 in ${ratio >= 1000 ? '1,000+' : ratio}`;
        } else {
          const ratio = pct > 0.05 ? Math.round(100 / pct) : 1000;
          rarityEl.textContent = `Bottom 1 in ${ratio >= 1000 ? '1,000+' : ratio}`;
        }
      }
    }

    if (rangeEl && inputEl) {
      rangeEl.addEventListener('input', () => {
        const val = parseInt(rangeEl.value, 10) || 100;
        inputEl.value = String(val);
        updateWidget(val);
      });
      inputEl.addEventListener('input', () => {
        const val = parseInt(inputEl.value, 10) || 100;
        rangeEl.value = String(val);
        updateWidget(val);
      });
    }

    updateWidget(118);
  });
</script>

    <!-- Deep Editorial Pillar Section 1: Detailed Society Profiles -->
    <article class="prose prose-zinc dark:prose-invert max-w-none space-y-8 text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed text-sm sm:text-base">
      <section class="space-y-4">
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white border-b border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 pb-2">
          Profiles of the Major High-IQ Societies
        </h2>
        <p>
          High-IQ societies are international membership associations formed to foster intellectual dialogue, publish multidisciplinary research journals, and provide social communities for individuals scoring at specific upper percentiles of standardized intelligence examinations. While Mensa is by far the largest and most well-known, specialized high-ceiling societies exist for scores reaching into the top fractions of one percent.
        </p>

        <div class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-6 not-prose my-6">
          <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/50 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
            <div class="flex items-center justify-between mb-2">
              <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">Mensa International (Top 2%)</h3>
              <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold px-2 py-0.5 rounded bg-blue-100 dark:bg-blue-950/60 text-blue-700 dark:text-blue-300">IQ 130+</span>
            </div>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mb-3">
              Founded in Oxford, England in 1946 by Roland Berrill and Dr. Lancelot Ware, Mensa was created to provide a society where race, nationality, religion, and social background were irrelevant. The sole entrance criterion is achieving a score at or above the 98th percentile on an approved intelligence test.
            </p>
            <ul class="text-xs space-y-1 text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400 list-disc list-inside">
              <li>Global membership spanning over 100 countries</li>
              <li>Hundreds of Special Interest Groups (SIGs)</li>
              <li>Annual Gatherings and academic research foundation grants</li>
            </ul>
          </div>

          <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/50 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
            <div class="flex items-center justify-between mb-2">
              <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">Intertel (Top 1%)</h3>
              <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold px-2 py-0.5 rounded bg-emerald-100 dark:bg-emerald-950/60 text-emerald-700 dark:text-emerald-300">IQ 135+</span>
            </div>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mb-3">
              Established in 1966 by Ralph Daniel, Intertel requires a qualifying score at or above the 99th percentile (IQ 135 on SD 15). The organization maintains an active international network and publishes the bi-monthly journal Integra, focusing on cognitive psychology and scientific inquiry.
            </p>
            <ul class="text-xs space-y-1 text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400 list-disc list-inside">
              <li>Selective 1 in 100 cutoff</li>
              <li>Close-knit regional chapters and annual general meetings</li>
              <li>Encourages research on giftedness and exceptional cognition</li>
            </ul>
          </div>

          <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/50 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
            <div class="flex items-center justify-between mb-2">
              <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">Triple Nine Society (Top 0.1%)</h3>
              <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold px-2 py-0.5 rounded bg-amber-100 dark:bg-amber-950/60 text-amber-700 dark:text-amber-300">IQ 146+</span>
            </div>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mb-3">
              Formed in 1978, the Triple Nine Society derives its name from its admission requirement: the 99.9th percentile (a cutoff of 146 on the SD 15 scale). Members represent roughly one out of every one thousand individuals in the general population.
            </p>
            <ul class="text-xs space-y-1 text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400 list-disc list-inside">
              <li>Highly decentralized, democratic governance</li>
              <li>Publishes the journal Vidya featuring member essays and original papers</li>
              <li>Does not accept high-range home tests; relies on strict standardized norms</li>
            </ul>
          </div>

          <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/50 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
            <div class="flex items-center justify-between mb-2">
              <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">Prometheus &amp; Mega Societies</h3>
              <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold px-2 py-0.5 rounded bg-rose-100 dark:bg-rose-950/60 text-rose-700 dark:text-rose-300">IQ 160+ / 171+</span>
            </div>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mb-3">
              The Prometheus Society (99.997th percentile, 1 in 30,000) and the Mega Society (99.9999th percentile, 1 in 1,000,000) represent the extreme outer tail of psychometric measurement. Standard clinical tests rarely differentiate reliably at this altitude due to limited sample sizes.
            </p>
            <ul class="text-xs space-y-1 text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400 list-disc list-inside">
              <li>Experimental high-ceiling psychometric batteries</li>
              <li>Small membership cohorts (dozens of members worldwide)</li>
              <li>Subject to ongoing debate regarding ceiling reliability and measurement error</li>
            </ul>
          </div>
        </div>
      </section>

      <!-- Deep Editorial Pillar Section 2: Accepted Prior Evidence & Qualifying Tests -->
      <section class="space-y-4">
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white border-b border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 pb-2">
          Accepted Qualifying Tests &amp; Prior Evidence Guidelines
        </h2>
        <p>
          Many candidates qualify for high-IQ societies without taking an official in-person admissions test through a process known as <strong>Prior Evidence</strong>. High-IQ organizations recognize standardized examinations administered during school or university admissions, provided the test possessed sufficient psychometric general factor (g-factor) saturation and standardized national norms during the administration year.
        </p>

        <div class="overflow-x-auto rounded-2xl border border-zinc-200/80 dark:border-zinc-800/80 my-6 not-prose">
          <table class="w-full text-left text-sm border-collapse">
            <thead class="bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900/70 text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 font-mono text-xs border-b border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
              <tr>
                <th class="p-3.5">Standardized Exam</th>
                <th class="p-3.5">Eligible Dates</th>
                <th class="p-3.5 text-center">Mensa Cutoff</th>
                <th class="p-3.5 text-center">Triple Nine Cutoff</th>
                <th class="p-3.5">Psychometric Context</th>
              </tr>
            </thead>
            <tbody class="divide-y divide-zinc-200/60 dark:divide-zinc-800/60 bg-white dark:bg-zinc-950/40 text-xs">
              {priorEvidenceTests.map((t) => (
                <tr class="hover:bg-zinc-50/60 dark:hover:bg-zinc-900/30 transition-colors">
                  <td class="p-3.5 font-semibold text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">
                    {t.exam}
                  </td>
                  <td class="p-3.5 font-mono text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400">
                    {t.period}
                  </td>
                  <td class="p-3.5 text-center font-mono font-bold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400">
                    {t.mensaCutoff}
                  </td>
                  <td class="p-3.5 text-center font-mono text-amber-600 dark:text-amber-400">
                    {t.tnsCutoff}
                  </td>
                  <td class="p-3.5 text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
                    {t.notes}
                  </td>
                </tr>
              ))}
            </tbody>
          </table>
        </div>

        <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-lg text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">
          Why Historical SAT and GRE Scores Ceased to Be Accepted
        </h3>
        <p>
          Before April 1995, the College Board's SAT was heavily saturated with the general cognitive factor ($g$). Psychometricians such as Douglas Detterman established that early SAT scores correlated between 0.82 and 0.86 with traditional IQ batteries like the WAIS. However, in 1995, the College Board recentered the SAT scale, altered the vocabulary section, and later added explicit curriculum-based math components, shifting the exam from an aptitude test toward an achievement battery. As a result, Mensa and other high-IQ societies ceased accepting SAT administrations taken after January 31, 1994 (or April 1995 depending on regional Mensa branch guidelines).
        </p>
      </section>

      <!-- Deep Editorial Pillar Section 3: Preparation & Testing Realities -->
      <section class="space-y-4">
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white border-b border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 pb-2">
          Preparation Strategies: Proctored vs Unproctored Testing
        </h2>
        <p>
          If you do not possess qualifying historical prior evidence, the primary path to high-IQ society membership is sitting for an official supervised admissions test. Understanding the structure of these examinations is essential for proper cognitive preparation:
        </p>
        <ul class="space-y-2">
          <li>
            <strong>Supervised Examination Batteries:</strong> Official Mensa testing typically consists of two distinct instruments: a non-verbal matrix reasoning assessment (such as the Raven's Advanced Progressive Matrices or the Cattell Culture Fair III) and a speeded general reasoning battery (such as the Wonderlic Personnel Test or the Reynolds Adaptable Intelligence Test - RAIT). Qualifying on either test satisfies the 98th percentile requirement.
          </li>
          <li>
            <strong>Practice Effects and Matrix Rule Induction:</strong> Non-verbal fluid reasoning items rely on specific transformation principles identified by cognitive researcher Patricia Carpenter (1990): constant progression, quantitative pairwise variations, figure intersection, and XOR/AND logic gates. Practicing these transformation rules helps eliminate test anxiety and minimizes mechanical unfamiliarity on test day.
          </li>
          <li>
            <strong>The Ceiling Effect &amp; Standard Error of Measurement:</strong> Standard clinical intelligence tests have a maximum ceiling around 145 to 160 IQ points. At scores exceeding three standard deviations above the mean, the number of norming subjects in standardization samples drops dramatically, causing the standard error of measurement (SEM) to expand from plus-or-minus 3 points up to plus-or-minus 8 points.
          </li>
        </ul>

        <!-- Internal Links Box -->
        <div class="not-prose p-6 rounded-2xl bg-gradient-to-r from-blue-900/10 via-indigo-900/10 to-blue-900/10 dark:from-blue-950/40 dark:via-indigo-950/40 dark:to-blue-950/40 border border-blue-200/80 dark:border-blue-800/80 my-6">
          <div class="flex flex-col sm:flex-row items-center justify-between gap-4">
            <div>
              <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-900 dark:text-white">
                Ready to Test Your Fluid Reasoning Ability?
              </h3>
              <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mt-1">
                Explore our high-difficulty matrix practice drills or take our standardized 24-item cognitive screening exam.
              </p>
            </div>
            <div class="flex flex-wrap gap-2 shrink-0">
              <a
                href="/mensa-iq-test-practice"
                class="px-4 py-2 rounded-xl text-xs font-semibold bg-blue-600 text-white hover:bg-blue-500 transition-colors shadow-xs"
              >
                Mensa Practice Drills
              </a>
              <a
                href="/iq-classification-scale"
                class="px-4 py-2 rounded-xl text-xs font-semibold bg-white dark:bg-zinc-800 text-zinc-900 dark:text-white border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-700 hover:bg-zinc-100 dark:hover:bg-zinc-700 transition-colors"
              >
                Classification Scale
              </a>
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </section>

      <!-- FAQ Section -->
      <section class="space-y-4 not-prose">
        <div class="border-b border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 pb-2">
          <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
            Frequently Asked Questions
          </h2>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400">
            Common questions regarding high-IQ societies, admission procedures, and score requirements.
          </p>
        </div>

        <div class="space-y-3">
          <details class="group p-4 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/70 dark:border-zinc-800/70">
            <summary class="font-heading font-semibold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white cursor-pointer flex justify-between items-center">
              <span>What IQ score is required to join Mensa?</span>
              <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform text-xs font-mono">&darr;</span>
            </summary>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
              Mensa membership requires scoring at or above the 98th percentile on an approved, supervised intelligence exam. On standard psychometric scales using a standard deviation of 15 (such as the WAIS-IV), this corresponds to an IQ score of 130 or higher. On scales using a standard deviation of 16 (Stanford-Binet), the threshold is 132, and on Cattell scales with SD 24, the threshold is 148.
            </p>
          </details>

          <details class="group p-4 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/70 dark:border-zinc-800/70">
            <summary class="font-heading font-semibold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white cursor-pointer flex justify-between items-center">
              <span>What is the difference between Mensa, Intertel, and Triple Nine Society?</span>
              <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform text-xs font-mono">&darr;</span>
            </summary>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
              The primary difference is the selectivity threshold: Mensa accepts the top 2 percent (98th percentile, IQ 130+ on SD 15); Intertel accepts the top 1 percent (99th percentile, IQ 135+ on SD 15); and the Triple Nine Society accepts the top 0.1 percent (99.9th percentile, IQ 146+ on SD 15).
            </p>
          </details>

          <details class="group p-4 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/70 dark:border-zinc-800/70">
            <summary class="font-heading font-semibold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white cursor-pointer flex justify-between items-center">
              <span>Can historical SAT or ACT test scores be used to join Mensa?</span>
              <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform text-xs font-mono">&darr;</span>
            </summary>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
              Yes, through prior evidence rules. American Mensa accepts qualifying scores on historical college entrance exams taken before specific dates, including the SAT taken before April 1995 (minimum 1250 verbal plus math, or 1300 before September 1974), the GRE taken before October 2001 (minimum 1250 combined), and the ACT taken before September 1989 (composite score of 29 or higher).
            </p>
          </details>

          <details class="group p-4 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/70 dark:border-zinc-800/70">
            <summary class="font-heading font-semibold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white cursor-pointer flex justify-between items-center">
              <span>What is the rarest and most selective high IQ society in the world?</span>
              <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform text-xs font-mono">&darr;</span>
            </summary>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
              The Prometheus Society requires scoring at the 99.997th percentile (IQ 160 on SD 15, representing 1 in 30,000 individuals). Even more selective is the Mega Society, which requires the 99.9999th percentile (IQ 171 on SD 15, or roughly 1 in 1,000,000 individuals), though measuring at such extreme ceilings carries significant psychometric measurement challenges.
            </p>
          </details>

          <details class="group p-4 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50/70 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/70 dark:border-zinc-800/70">
            <summary class="font-heading font-semibold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white cursor-pointer flex justify-between items-center">
              <span>Can an online IQ test qualify someone for Mensa membership?</span>
              <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform text-xs font-mono">&darr;</span>
            </summary>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
              No unproctored online test qualifies directly for official high-IQ society membership. Formal admission requires either sitting for an in-person, proctored admissions battery administered by Mensa or a certified psychologist, or submitting verified transcripts from recognized clinical or academic standardized examinations.
            </p>
          </details>
        </div>
      </section>

  <!-- Client-side Interactive Widget Script -->
  <script is:inline>
    (function () {
      const slider = document.getElementById('society-score-slider');
      const input = document.getElementById('society-score-input');
      const displayPct = document.getElementById('display-percentile');
      const displayFreq = document.getElementById('display-frequency');
      const listEl = document.getElementById('society-badges-list');

      if (!slider || !input || !displayPct || !displayFreq || !listEl) return;

      const societyData = [
        { name: 'Mensa International', minIQ: 130, percentile: 'Top 2%', rarity: '1 in 50' },
        { name: 'Intertel', minIQ: 135, percentile: 'Top 1%', rarity: '1 in 100' },
        { name: 'Colloquy Society', minIQ: 140, percentile: 'Top 0.5%', rarity: '1 in 200' },
        { name: 'Triple Nine Society', minIQ: 146, percentile: 'Top 0.1%', rarity: '1 in 1,000' },
        { name: 'Prometheus Society', minIQ: 160, percentile: 'Top 0.003%', rarity: '1 in 33,333' }
      ];

      // Error function approximation for cumulative normal distribution
      function erf(x) {
        const a1 = 0.254829592, a2 = -0.284496736, a3 = 1.421413741;
        const a4 = -1.453152027, a5 = 1.061405429, p = 0.3275911;
        const sign = x < 0 ? -1 : 1;
        x = Math.abs(x);
        const t = 1.0 / (1.0 + p * x);
        const y = 1.0 - (((((a5 * t + a4) * t) + a3) * t + a2) * t + a1) * t * Math.exp(-x * x);
        return sign * y;
      }

      function update(score) {
        slider.value = score;
        input.value = score;

        const z = (score - 100) / 15;
        const cdf = 0.5 * (1 + erf(z / Math.SQRT2));
        const pct = Math.min(99.999, Math.max(0.001, cdf * 100));
        const topFraction = 1 - cdf;
        const freqRatio = topFraction > 0 ? Math.round(1 / topFraction) : 1000000;

        displayPct.textContent = pct >= 99.9 ? pct.toFixed(2) + 'th Percentile' : pct.toFixed(1) + 'th Percentile';
        displayFreq.textContent = '1 in ' + freqRatio.toLocaleString() + ' people';

        // Render society qualifications
        listEl.innerHTML = societyData.map(s => {
          const qualified = score >= s.minIQ;
          const diff = s.minIQ - score;
          let statusHtml = '';

          if (qualified) {
            statusHtml = '<span class="px-2 py-0.5 rounded text-[11px] font-semibold bg-emerald-100 dark:bg-emerald-950/70 text-emerald-800 dark:text-emerald-300">Qualifies (Score ' + score + ' >= ' + s.minIQ + ')</span>';
          } else if (diff <= 5) {
            statusHtml = '<span class="px-2 py-0.5 rounded text-[11px] font-semibold bg-amber-100 dark:bg-amber-950/70 text-amber-800 dark:text-amber-300">Target Range (' + diff + ' pts to cutoff ' + s.minIQ + ')</span>';
          } else {
            statusHtml = '<span class="px-2 py-0.5 rounded text-[11px] text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">Requires IQ ' + s.minIQ + ' (' + diff + ' pts above)</span>';
          }

          return '<li class="flex items-center justify-between p-2 rounded-lg bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900/60 border border-zinc-100 dark:border-zinc-800/60">' +
            '<div><span class="font-medium text-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-200">' + s.name + '</span> <span class="text-zinc-400 font-mono text-[11px]">(' + s.percentile + ')</span></div>' +
            statusHtml +
            '</li>';
        }).join('');
      }

      slider.addEventListener('input', (e) => {
        update(parseInt(e.target.value, 10) || 130);
      });

      input.addEventListener('change', (e) => {
        let val = parseInt(e.target.value, 10);
        if (isNaN(val)) val = 130;
        val = Math.min(165, Math.max(115, val));
        update(val);
      });

      update(132);
    })();
  </script>
</Layout>

        Frequently Asked Questions: IQ Scores &amp; Distributions
      </h2>

      <div class="space-y-3">
        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <summary class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-zinc-100 cursor-pointer list-none flex items-center justify-between">
            <span>What is considered an average IQ score?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
            On standardized intelligence tests using a standard deviation of 15, an average score is defined as 100. By statistical design, approximately 50% of the population scores between 90 and 109, and about 68.2% scores between 85 and 115 (within one standard deviation of the mean).
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <summary class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-zinc-100 cursor-pointer list-none flex items-center justify-between">
            <span>What score is required to join Mensa?</span>
            <summary-arrow />
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
            Mensa membership requires scoring at or above the 98th percentile on an approved, proctored intelligence assessment. On tests with a standard deviation of 15 (such as the WAIS), this corresponds to a score of 130 or higher. On tests with an SD of 16 (Stanford-Binet), it corresponds to 132.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <summary class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-zinc-100 cursor-pointer list-none flex items-center justify-between">
            <span>What is the difference between SD 15 and SD 16?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 mt-3 leading-relaxed">
            The standard deviation defines the mathematical spread of scores. Under SD 15, two standard deviations above the mean equals 130 (100 + 2 &times; 15). Under SD 16, two standard deviations equals 132 (100 + 2 &times; 16). Both scores reflect the exact same percentile rank (97.72nd percentile).
          </p>
        </details>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Mathematical Formula Breakdown -->
    <section class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
      <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
        Mathematical Formulation: Z-Scores and CDF Integration
      </h3>
      <p class="mb-3">
        The conversion from standard score to percentile operates through standard normal standardization:
      </p>
      <div class="p-4 rounded-xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 font-mono text-xs mb-3">
        z = (IQ - 100) / 15<br />
        Percentile = &Phi;(z) &times; 100 = 0.5 &times; [1 + erf(z / &radic;2)] &times; 100
      </div>
      <p>
        Where &Phi; is the cumulative distribution function of the standard normal distribution, and erf is the Gauss error function.
      </p>
    </section>

    <!-- Frequently Asked Questions Section -->
    <section class="space-y-6">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Frequently Asked Questions: Percentiles &amp; Normal Distribution
        </h2>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
          Essential concepts on Gaussian distributions, z-scores, and percentile interpretation.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="space-y-3">
        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is an IQ percentile?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            An IQ percentile indicates the percentage of the general population that scores at or below a given IQ score on a standardized normal distribution (Mean 100, SD 15).
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What percentile is an IQ of 100?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            An IQ of 100 is the exact median of the population, corresponding to the 50th percentile.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What IQ score is needed to qualify for Mensa?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Mensa requires a score at or above the 98th percentile, which corresponds to an IQ of 130 or higher on the Wechsler scale (SD 15) or 132 on the Stanford-Binet scale (SD 16).
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>How is the IQ percentile calculated mathematically?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            IQ percentiles are derived using the standard normal cumulative distribution function (CDF). First, the Z-score is calculated as z = (IQ - 100) / 15, then integrated through the Gaussian error function.
          </p>
        </details>
      </div>
    </section>
