<script>
  // Mathematical functions
  function erf(x: number): number {
    const sign = x >= 0 ? 1 : -1;
    const absX = Math.abs(x);
    const a1 = 0.254829592;
    const a2 = -0.284496736;
    const a3 = 1.421413741;
    const a4 = -1.453152027;
    const a5 = 1.061405429;
    const p = 0.3275911;
    const t = 1.0 / (1.0 + p * absX);
    const y = 1.0 - (((((a5 * t + a4) * t + a3) * t + a2) * t + a1) * t) * Math.exp(-absX * absX);
    return sign * y;
  }

  function normalCdf(z: number): number {
    return 0.5 * (1.0 + erf(z / Math.SQRT2));
  }

  // Inverse normal approximation (Beasley-Springer-Moro)
  function rationalApproximation(t: number): number {
    const c = [2.515517, 0.802853, 0.010328];
    const d = [1.432788, 0.189269, 0.001308];
    return t - ((c[2] * t + c[1]) * t + c[0]) / (((d[2] * t + d[1]) * t + d[0]) * t + 1.0);
  }

  function invNormalCdf(p: number): number {
    if (p <= 0) return -4.0;
    if (p >= 1) return 4.0;
    if (p < 0.5) {
      return -rationalApproximation(Math.sqrt(-2.0 * Math.log(p)));
    } else {
      return rationalApproximation(Math.sqrt(-2.0 * Math.log(1.0 - p)));
    }
  }

  document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
    // Mode Switching
    const tabIqToPct = document.getElementById('tab-iq-to-pct');
    const tabPctToIq = document.getElementById('tab-pct-to-iq');
    const panelIqToPct = document.getElementById('panel-iq-to-pct');
    const panelPctToIq = document.getElementById('panel-pct-to-iq');

    if (tabIqToPct && tabPctToIq && panelIqToPct && panelPctToIq) {
      tabIqToPct.addEventListener('click', () => {
        panelIqToPct.classList.remove('hidden');
        panelPctToIq.classList.add('hidden');
        tabIqToPct.className = 'flex-1 py-2 px-4 rounded-xl text-xs sm:text-sm font-semibold transition-all bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 text-zinc-950 dark:text-white shadow-xs';
        tabPctToIq.className = 'flex-1 py-2 px-4 rounded-xl text-xs sm:text-sm font-medium transition-all text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 hover:text-zinc-900 dark:hover:text-white';
      });

      tabPctToIq.addEventListener('click', () => {
        panelPctToIq.classList.remove('hidden');
        panelIqToPct.classList.add('hidden');
        tabPctToIq.className = 'flex-1 py-2 px-4 rounded-xl text-xs sm:text-sm font-semibold transition-all bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 text-zinc-950 dark:text-white shadow-xs';
        tabIqToPct.className = 'flex-1 py-2 px-4 rounded-xl text-xs sm:text-sm font-medium transition-all text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 hover:text-zinc-900 dark:hover:text-white';
      });
    }

    // Panel 1: IQ -> Percentile
    const iqInput = document.getElementById('iq-input') as HTMLInputElement;
    const iqSlider = document.getElementById('iq-slider') as HTMLInputElement;
    const outputPercentile = document.getElementById('output-percentile');
    const outputRarity = document.getElementById('output-rarity');
    const outputZscore = document.getElementById('output-zscore');
    const outputClassification = document.getElementById('output-classification');

    function updateFromIq(iq: number) {
      const z = (iq - 100) / 15;
      const pct = Math.min(99.99, Math.max(0.01, normalCdf(z) * 100));

      if (outputPercentile) outputPercentile.textContent = `${pct.toFixed(1)}%`;
      if (outputZscore) outputZscore.textContent = `${z >= 0 ? '+' : ''}${z.toFixed(2)} σ`;

      // Rarity
      if (outputRarity) {
        if (pct >= 50) {
          const rem = 100 - pct;
          const ratio = rem > 0.05 ? Math.round(100 / rem) : 1000;
          outputRarity.textContent = `Top ${(rem).toFixed(1)}% (1 in ${ratio >= 1000 ? '1,000+' : ratio} people)`;
        } else {
          const ratio = pct > 0.05 ? Math.round(100 / pct) : 1000;
          outputRarity.textContent = `Bottom ${pct.toFixed(1)}% (1 in ${ratio} people)`;
        }
      }

      // Classification
      if (outputClassification) {
        if (iq >= 145) outputClassification.textContent = 'Profoundly Superior Ability';
        else if (iq >= 130) outputClassification.textContent = 'Very Superior (Mensa Cutoff)';
        else if (iq >= 120) outputClassification.textContent = 'Superior Cognitive Ability';
        else if (iq >= 110) outputClassification.textContent = 'High Average Ability';
        else if (iq >= 90) outputClassification.textContent = 'Average Cognitive Ability';
        else if (iq >= 80) outputClassification.textContent = 'Low Average Ability';
        else outputClassification.textContent = 'Borderline Range';
      }
    }

    if (iqInput && iqSlider) {
      iqInput.addEventListener('input', () => {
        const val = parseInt(iqInput.value, 10) || 100;
        iqSlider.value = String(val);
        updateFromIq(val);
      });
      iqSlider.addEventListener('input', () => {
        const val = parseInt(iqSlider.value, 10);
        iqInput.value = String(val);
        updateFromIq(val);
      });
    }

    // Panel 2: Percentile -> IQ
    const pctInput = document.getElementById('pct-input') as HTMLInputElement;
    const pctSlider = document.getElementById('pct-slider') as HTMLInputElement;
    const outputIqFromPct = document.getElementById('output-iq-from-pct');
    const outputSdFromPct = document.getElementById('output-sd-from-pct');
    const outputMensaStatus = document.getElementById('output-mensa-status');

    function updateFromPct(pct: number) {
      const p = pct / 100;
      const z = invNormalCdf(p);
      const iq = Math.round(100 + 15 * z);

      if (outputIqFromPct) outputIqFromPct.textContent = `${iq} IQ`;
      if (outputSdFromPct) outputSdFromPct.textContent = `${z >= 0 ? '+' : ''}${z.toFixed(2)} σ (Wechsler SD 15)`;

      if (outputMensaStatus) {
        if (pct >= 98.0) {
          outputMensaStatus.textContent = 'Qualifies for Mensa (Top 2%)';
          outputMensaStatus.className = 'text-xs font-semibold px-3 py-1 rounded-full bg-indigo-100 text-indigo-800 dark:bg-indigo-950 dark:text-indigo-300';
        } else {
          const ptsNeeded = Math.max(1, 130 - iq);
          outputMensaStatus.textContent = `Below 98th Percentile (Need +${ptsNeeded} IQ pts)`;
          outputMensaStatus.className = 'text-xs font-semibold px-3 py-1 rounded-full bg-zinc-100 text-zinc-700 dark:bg-zinc-800 dark:text-zinc-300';
        }
      }
    }

    if (pctInput && pctSlider) {
      pctInput.addEventListener('input', () => {
        const val = parseFloat(pctInput.value) || 50;
        pctSlider.value = String(val);
        updateFromPct(val);
      });
      pctSlider.addEventListener('input', () => {
        const val = parseFloat(pctSlider.value);
        pctInput.value = String(val);
        updateFromPct(val);
      });
    }

    // Initial run
    updateFromIq(120);
    updateFromPct(98.0);
  });
</script>

      <div class="flex flex-col sm:flex-row items-start sm:items-center justify-between gap-2 mb-6">
        <div>
          <h2 class="font-heading text-xl sm:text-2xl font-bold text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
            Fluid Intelligence (Gf) vs. Crystallized Intelligence (Gc)
          </h2>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">

      <!-- Vector Line Chart SVG -->
      <div class="w-full overflow-x-auto pb-4">

          <svg viewBox="0 0 600 240" class="w-full h-auto select-none" fill="none" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">
            <!-- Grid Lines -->
            <line x1="50" y1="40" x2="550" y2="40" stroke="#e4e4e7" class="dark:stroke-zinc-800" stroke-width="1" />
            <line x1="50" y1="90" x2="550" y2="90" stroke="#e4e4e7" class="dark:stroke-zinc-800" stroke-width="1" />
            <line x1="50" y1="140" x2="550" y2="140" stroke="#e4e4e7" class="dark:stroke-zinc-800" stroke-width="1" />
            <line x1="50" y1="190" x2="550" y2="190" stroke="#d4d4d8" class="dark:stroke-zinc-700" stroke-width="2" />

            <!-- X-Axis Age Ticks -->
            <!-- 20s (x=100), 30s (x=180), 40s (x=260), 50s (x=340), 60s (x=420), 70s+ (x=500) -->
            <text x="100" y="212" font-family="monospace" font-size="11" fill="#71717a" text-anchor="middle">Age 20</text>
            <text x="180" y="212" font-family="monospace" font-size="11" fill="#71717a" text-anchor="middle">Age 30</text>
            <text x="260" y="212" font-family="monospace" font-size="11" fill="#71717a" text-anchor="middle">Age 40</text>
            <text x="340" y="212" font-family="monospace" font-size="11" fill="#71717a" text-anchor="middle">Age 50</text>
            <text x="420" y="212" font-family="monospace" font-size="11" fill="#71717a" text-anchor="middle">Age 60</text>
            <text x="500" y="212" font-family="monospace" font-size="11" fill="#71717a" text-anchor="middle">Age 70+</text>

            <!-- Gf Curve: Peaks at age 22, then steady gradual slope downward -->
            <path d="M 60 110 C 90 45, 120 45, 180 65 C 260 90, 340 120, 420 145 C 460 160, 500 175, 540 185" stroke="#2563eb" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" fill="none" />

            <!-- Gc Curve: Climbs steadily through 30s and 40s, peaks in late 50s, remains high -->
            <path d="M 60 140 C 120 115, 180 90, 260 70 C 340 55, 400 50, 440 55 C 480 65, 510 80, 540 95" stroke="#059669" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" fill="none" />

            <!-- Peak Callout Markers -->
            <circle cx="110" cy="48" r="5" fill="#2563eb" stroke="#ffffff" stroke-width="2" />
            <rect x="70" y="20" width="80" height="18" rx="6" fill="#2563eb" />
            <text x="110" y="32" font-family="monospace" font-size="10" font-weight="bold" fill="#ffffff" text-anchor="middle">Gf Peak (~22)</text>

            <circle cx="420" cy="53" r="5" fill="#059669" stroke="#ffffff" stroke-width="2" />
            <rect x="380" y="25" width="80" height="18" rx="6" fill="#059669" />
            <text x="420" y="37" font-family="monospace" font-size="10" font-weight="bold" fill="#ffffff" text-anchor="middle">Gc Peak (~58)</text>
          </svg>
        </div>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- The Age-Norming Paradox Section -->
    <section class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-6">
      <div class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between">
        <div>
          <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
            The Fundamental Rule
          </span>
          <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
            Why is the Average IQ Always 100?
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
            A common misconception is that an older adult has a lower IQ than a 25-year-old. In professional psychometrics, IQ is an age-normed relative ranking, not an absolute raw count.
          </p>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
            Many standardized cognitive batteries calibrate scores relative to peers within specified age brackets, setting the normative mean score to 100 in each reference group.
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between">
        <div>
          <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-emerald-600 dark:text-emerald-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
            Cognitive Dynamics
          </span>
          <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
            What Actually Changes with Age?
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
            While standardized IQ remains constant, the underlying balance of cognitive faculties shifts:
          </p>
          <ul class="space-y-2 text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300">
            <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
              <span class="text-blue-600 font-bold">&bull;</span>
              <span><strong>Processing Speed &amp; Fluid Logic:</strong> Visual reaction speeds and novel matrix induction peak in early adulthood.</span>
            </li>
            <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
              <span class="text-emerald-600 font-bold">&bull;</span>
              <span><strong>Verbal &amp; Crystallized Acumen:</strong> Vocabulary, verbal analogies, and domain expertise expand through the 5th and 6th decades.</span>
            </li>
          </ul>
        </div>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- The Flynn Effect Section -->
    <section class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
      <div class="max-w-3xl">
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Generational Shift
        </span>
        <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          The Flynn Effect: Are Subsequent Generations Scoring Higher?
        </h3>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
          Named after psychometric researcher James Flynn, the Flynn effect describes the documented rise in raw cognitive test performance observed across industrialized nations throughout the 20th century (approximately 3 IQ points per decade).
        </p>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Because raw performance shifted upward over time due to enhanced nutrition, ubiquitous formal schooling, and technological complexity, test publishers periodically re-standardize test norms over time to maintain a standardized mean of 100.
        </p>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Frequently Asked Questions Section -->
    <section class="space-y-6">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Frequently Asked Questions: Age &amp; Cognitive Trajectories
        </h2>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
          Scientific consensus on age-norming, cognitive peaks, and generational score drift.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="space-y-3">
        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Why is the average IQ score 100 at every age?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Because IQ tests are standardized with age-normed cohorts. A raw score obtained by a 20-year-old is compared only against other 20-year-olds, and a raw score from a 60-year-old is compared against other 60-year-olds, fixing the median of every age cohort at 100.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>At what age does fluid intelligence peak?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Fluid reasoning (Gf) typically peaks in early adulthood (ages 20 to 25) and gradually declines thereafter as biological processing speed and neural efficiency shift.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Does crystallized intelligence decline with age?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Unlike fluid intelligence, crystallized intelligence (Gc) representing vocabulary, verbal knowledge, and general comprehension remains stable or continues to increase through the 50s and 60s.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is the Flynn Effect?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            The Flynn effect refers to the secular generational increase in raw test scores observed across the 20th century (roughly 3 points per decade), requiring psychologists to recalibrate test batteries every 10 to 15 years.
          </p>
        </details>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Assessment CTA -->
    <section class="p-8 sm:p-12 rounded-3xl bg-gradient-to-tr from-blue-600 via-indigo-600 to-purple-600 text-white text-center shadow-lg relative overflow-hidden">
      <h2 class="font-heading font-extrabold text-2xl sm:text-4xl mb-3 tracking-tight">
        Test Your Non-Verbal Fluid Reasoning
      </h2>
      <p class="text-blue-100 text-sm sm:text-base max-w-xl mx-auto mb-6 leading-relaxed">
        Our 24-item assessment evaluates non-verbal fluid reasoning and visual pattern logic. Complete the timed assessment to see your estimated score range.
      </p>
      <a 
        href="/test" 
        class="inline-flex items-center gap-2 px-8 py-4 rounded-full text-base font-bold text-zinc-950 bg-white hover:bg-zinc-100 transition-all shadow-md hover:scale-102 active:scale-98"
      >
        <span>Start Free Assessment</span>
        <svg class="w-5 h-5" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5">
          <path stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" d="M13.5 4.5L21 12m0 0l-7.5 7.5M21 12H3" />
        </svg>
      </a>
    </section>

  </main>

  <Footer />
</Layout>

<Layout title={title} description={description} schema={schema}>
  <Header currentPath="/matrix-reasoning-test" />

  <main class="flex-1 max-w-5xl w-full mx-auto px-4 sm:px-6 py-8 sm:py-12 flex flex-col gap-12">
    
    <!-- Hero Header -->
    <header class="text-center max-w-3xl mx-auto">
      <div class="inline-flex items-center gap-2 px-3.5 py-1.5 rounded-full bg-blue-50 dark:bg-blue-950/50 border border-blue-200 dark:border-blue-900 text-xs font-mono font-medium text-blue-700 dark:text-blue-300 mb-4">
        <span>NON-VERBAL REASONING &bull; CARPENTER TAXONOMY</span>
      </div>
      <h1 class="font-heading font-extrabold text-3xl sm:text-5xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white tracking-tight leading-tight mb-4">
        Matrix Reasoning Test Guide &amp; Core Rules
      </h1>
      <p class="text-base sm:text-lg text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
        Matrix reasoning is widely considered the gold standard for measuring non-verbal Fluid Intelligence (Gf). Discover the 5 fundamental mathematical transformation rules that govern all progressive matrix puzzles.
      </p>
    </header>

    <!-- Why Matrix Reasoning Isolates Gf Section -->
    <section class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50/80 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/80 dark:border-zinc-800/80 shadow-xs">
      <div class="max-w-3xl">
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Theoretical Foundation
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          Why Matrix Reasoning Is Used to Assess Fluid Reasoning
        </h2>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
          Unlike verbal analogies or vocabulary subtests which heavily reflect formal education and cultural background, progressive matrices use abstract geometric figures to isolate inductive problem-solving capacity.
        </p>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Matrix reasoning tasks are widely used to assess fluid reasoning because they require identifying relationships, rules, and patterns in novel visual information without reliance on language.
        </p>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- The 5 Core Transformation Rules (Carpenter, Just & Shell, 1990) -->
    <section class="space-y-6">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading text-xl sm:text-2xl font-bold text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
          The 5 Universal Matrix Transformation Rules
        </h2>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
          Almost every matrix problem in clinical and unproctored testing is constructed using one or more of these 5 formalized rule systems.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-2 gap-6">
        
        <!-- Rule 1: Constant Progression -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between">
          <div>
            <div class="w-8 h-8 rounded-xl bg-blue-100 dark:bg-blue-950 text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 font-mono font-bold text-sm flex items-center justify-center mb-3">
              1
            </div>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
              Constant in a Row / Column
            </h3>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
              A specific geometric attribute remains completely invariant across a horizontal row, while another attribute changes systematically down columns.
            </p>
          </div>
          <div class="p-3 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800/60 text-xs font-mono text-zinc-500">
            Example: Row 1 is always squares; Row 2 circles; Row 3 triangles.
          </div>
        </div>

        <!-- Rule 2: Quantitative Progression -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between">
          <div>
            <div class="w-8 h-8 rounded-xl bg-indigo-100 dark:bg-indigo-950 text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 font-mono font-bold text-sm flex items-center justify-center mb-3">
              2
            </div>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
              Quantitative Arithmetic Progression
            </h3>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
              The number, size, or angle of elements increases or decreases by a fixed arithmetic increment (+1, +2, +45 degrees) at each sequential step.
            </p>
          </div>
          <div class="p-3 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800/60 text-xs font-mono text-zinc-500">
            Example: 1 dot &rarr; 2 dots &rarr; 3 dots across the row.
          </div>
        </div>

        <!-- Rule 3: Boolean XOR Logic -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between">
          <div>
            <div class="w-8 h-8 rounded-xl bg-purple-100 dark:bg-purple-950 text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 font-mono font-bold text-sm flex items-center justify-center mb-3">
              3
            </div>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
              Boolean XOR Logic (Overlap Cancellation)
            </h3>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
              Columns 1 and 2 are superimposed. Any line segment present in both cells cancels out and vanishes. Only unique lines persist into Column 3.
            </p>
          </div>
          <div class="p-3 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800/60 text-xs font-mono text-zinc-500">
            Formula: Cell 3 = Cell 1 XOR Cell 2.
          </div>
        </div>

        <!-- Rule 4: Latin Square Permutation -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between">
          <div>
            <div class="w-8 h-8 rounded-xl bg-teal-100 dark:bg-teal-950 text-teal-600 dark:text-teal-400 font-mono font-bold text-sm flex items-center justify-center mb-3">
              4
            </div>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
              Distribution of Three (Latin Square)
            </h3>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
              Three distinct attributes (e.g. circle, square, triangle) must appear exactly once in every row and exactly once in every column.
            </p>
          </div>
          <div class="p-3 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800/60 text-xs font-mono text-zinc-500">
            Rule: Sum of each row and column contains attributes A, B, and C.
          </div>
        </div>

        <!-- Rule 5: Spatial Rotation & Reflection -->
        <div class="p-6 rounded-3xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 shadow-xs flex flex-col justify-between md:col-span-2">
          <div>
            <div class="w-8 h-8 rounded-xl bg-amber-100 dark:bg-amber-950 text-amber-600 dark:text-amber-400 font-mono font-bold text-sm flex items-center justify-center mb-3">
              5
            </div>
            <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
              Spatial Rotation &amp; Reflection
            </h3>
            <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mb-4">
              Geometric elements undergo fixed angular rotations (e.g., 45&deg; or 90&deg; clockwise or counter-clockwise), or mirror reflections across horizontal or vertical symmetry axes.
            </p>
          </div>
          <div class="p-3 rounded-xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800/60 text-xs font-mono text-zinc-500">
            Example: Arrow rotates 90&deg; clockwise at each step: Up &rarr; Right &rarr; Down.
          </div>
        </div>

      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Interactive Demonstration Puzzle Card -->
    <section class="p-6 sm:p-10 rounded-3xl bg-zinc-50/80 dark:bg-zinc-900/40 border border-zinc-200/80 dark:border-zinc-800/80 shadow-xs">
      <div class="max-w-2xl mx-auto text-center mb-6">
        <span class="text-xs font-mono font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 uppercase tracking-wider block mb-2">
          Practice Sample
        </span>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
          Interactive Matrix Problem: Boolean XOR in Action
        </h2>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400 mt-1">
          Examine the line interactions across Row 1 and Row 2. Which option completes Row 3?
        </p>
      </div>

      <!-- Sample Matrix Vector Stimulus -->
      <div class="max-w-xs mx-auto p-4 rounded-2xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 mb-6">
        <svg viewBox="0 0 280 280" class="w-full h-auto select-none" fill="none" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg">
          <rect width="280" height="280" rx="8" fill="#f4f4f5" class="dark:fill-zinc-900" stroke="#e4e4e7" class="dark:stroke-zinc-800" stroke-width="2" />
          <line x1="93.3" y1="0" x2="93.3" y2="280" stroke="#d4d4d8" class="dark:stroke-zinc-700" stroke-width="2" />
          <line x1="186.6" y1="0" x2="186.6" y2="280" stroke="#d4d4d8" class="dark:stroke-zinc-700" stroke-width="2" />
          <line x1="0" y1="93.3" x2="280" y2="93.3" stroke="#d4d4d8" class="dark:stroke-zinc-700" stroke-width="2" />
          <line x1="0" y1="186.6" x2="280" y2="186.6" stroke="#d4d4d8" class="dark:stroke-zinc-700" stroke-width="2" />

          <!-- Row 1: Vertical + Horizontal = Cross -->
          <line x1="46.6" y1="20" x2="46.6" y2="73.3" stroke="#2563eb" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="113.3" y1="46.6" x2="166.6" y2="46.6" stroke="#2563eb" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="233.3" y1="20" x2="233.3" y2="73.3" stroke="#2563eb" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="206.6" y1="46.6" x2="260" y2="46.6" stroke="#2563eb" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />

          <!-- Row 2: Overlapping horizontal cancels -->
          <line x1="20" y1="140" x2="73.3" y2="140" stroke="#6366f1" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="46.6" y1="113.3" x2="46.6" y2="166.6" stroke="#6366f1" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="113.3" y1="140" x2="166.6" y2="140" stroke="#6366f1" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <!-- Result: horizontal cancelled, only vertical remains -->
          <line x1="233.3" y1="113.3" x2="233.3" y2="166.6" stroke="#6366f1" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />

          <!-- Row 3: Diagonal X lines -->
          <line x1="20" y1="206.6" x2="73.3" y2="260" stroke="#a855f7" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="113.3" y1="206.6" x2="166.6" y2="260" stroke="#a855f7" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />
          <line x1="166.6" y1="206.6" x2="113.3" y2="260" stroke="#a855f7" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" />

          <!-- Missing Question Mark Cell -->
          <g transform="translate(186.6, 186.6)">
            <rect width="93.3" height="93.3" fill="#eff6ff" class="dark:fill-blue-950/40" stroke="#3b82f6" stroke-width="2" stroke-dasharray="4 3" />
            <text x="46.6" y="58" font-family="sans-serif" font-size="36" font-weight="bold" fill="#2563eb" text-anchor="middle">?</text>
          </g>
        </svg>
      </div>

      <!-- Toggle Solution Details Button -->
      <div class="text-center">
        <button
          id="btn-toggle-solution"
          type="button"
          class="px-6 py-2.5 rounded-full text-xs sm:text-sm font-semibold text-blue-600 dark:text-blue-400 bg-blue-50 dark:bg-blue-950/50 border border-blue-200 dark:border-blue-900 hover:bg-blue-100 transition-colors"
        >
          Reveal Step-by-Step Solution &darr;
        </button>
      </div>

      <!-- Hidden Solution Explanation Drawer -->
      <div id="solution-drawer" class="hidden max-w-xl mx-auto mt-6 p-6 rounded-2xl bg-white dark:bg-zinc-950 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed space-y-3">
        <h4 class="font-heading font-bold text-base text-zinc-950 dark:text-white">
          Solution Breakdown:
        </h4>
        <p>
          1. <strong>Analyze the Transformation Rule:</strong> In Row 1, Column 1 has a vertical segment and Column 2 has a horizontal segment. Both are unique, so both appear in Column 3.
        </p>
        <p>
          2. <strong>Identify Cancellation:</strong> In Row 2, both Column 1 and Column 2 contain a horizontal segment. Because it is present in both cells, the Boolean XOR operation cancels it out. Only the vertical line survives.
        </p>
        <p>
          3. <strong>Apply to Row 3:</strong> Column 1 contains a forward diagonal (\). Column 2 contains both a forward diagonal (\) and a backward diagonal (/). The forward diagonal cancels out. What remains is a single backward diagonal line (/).
        </p>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Frequently Asked Questions Section -->
    <section class="space-y-6">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-xl sm:text-2xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          Frequently Asked Questions: Matrix Reasoning
        </h2>
        <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-500 dark:text-zinc-400">
          Key insights into non-verbal testing, transformation logic, and preparation strategies.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="space-y-3">
        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What is a matrix reasoning test?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            A matrix reasoning test is a non-verbal cognitive assessment where test-takers examine a 2x2 or 3x3 grid of abstract shapes and identify the missing piece that completes the underlying transformation pattern.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>What are Carpenter's 5 rules for progressive matrices?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            The 5 rules documented by Carpenter, Just, and Shell (1990) are: constant in a row, quantitative progression, distribution of three values, distribution of two values (Boolean XOR cancellation), and spatial movement/rotation.
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Why is matrix reasoning considered culture-reduced?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Because it uses language-free geometric stimuli, matrix reasoning minimizes educational, linguistic, and socioeconomic biases, isolating pure fluid intelligence (Gf).
          </p>
        </details>

        <details class="group p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 transition-colors">
          <summary class="flex items-center justify-between cursor-pointer font-heading font-bold text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white list-none">
            <span>Can you prepare or practice for matrix reasoning tests?</span>
            <span class="text-zinc-400 group-open:rotate-180 transition-transform">&darr;</span>
          </summary>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed mt-3 pt-3 border-t border-zinc-200/60 dark:border-zinc-800">
            Yes. Learning to systematically decompose independent attributes (shape, count, orientation, fill) and checking rows and columns separately significantly improves accuracy and response speed.
          </p>
        </details>
      </div>
    </section>

    <!-- Assessment CTA -->
    <section class="p-8 sm:p-12 rounded-3xl bg-gradient-to-tr from-blue-600 via-indigo-600 to-purple-600 text-white text-center shadow-lg relative overflow-hidden">
      <h2 class="font-heading font-extrabold text-2xl sm:text-4xl mb-3 tracking-tight">
        Put Your Matrix Skills to the Test
      </h2>
      <p class="text-blue-100 text-sm sm:text-base max-w-xl mx-auto mb-6 leading-relaxed">
        Our active 24-question test includes 12 progressive matrix puzzles spanning Boolean XOR logic, Latin square distributions, and quantitative additions.
      </p>
      <a 
        href="/test" 
        class="inline-flex items-center gap-2 px-8 py-4 rounded-full text-base font-bold text-zinc-950 bg-white hover:bg-zinc-100 transition-all shadow-md hover:scale-102 active:scale-98"
      >

  const btnToggleSolution = document.getElementById('btn-toggle-solution');
  const solutionDrawer = document.getElementById('solution-drawer');
  if (btnToggleSolution && solutionDrawer) {
    btnToggleSolution.addEventListener('click', () => {
      const isHidden = solutionDrawer.classList.toggle('hidden');
      btnToggleSolution.textContent = isHidden ? 'Reveal Step-by-Step Solution ↓' : 'Hide Solution ↑';
    });

          <p class="mt-1">Line segments inside the frame superimpose and cancel out when overlapping across column transitions.</p>

          <strong class="text-zinc-900 dark:text-white font-semibold">Layer 3: Directional Enantiomer Inversion</strong>
          <p class="mt-1">The orientation of asymmetric corner notches flips polarity based on whether the row parity is odd or even.</p>

      domainSubtitle="Mental Rotation, 3D Folding Nets & Orthographic Logic"
      domainDescription="Visual-Spatial Processing (Gv) assesses your capacity to perceive, analyze, synthesize, and transform visual patterns and spatial configurations. It serves as a vital cognitive predictor for engineering, architecture, mathematics, and spatial navigation."
      testDurationMinutes={15}
      itemCount={16}
      gradientClass="from-indigo-600 to-purple-600"
      badgeClass="bg-indigo-100 text-indigo-800 dark:bg-indigo-950/60 dark:text-indigo-300 border-indigo-200 dark:border-indigo-800"
    />

    <!-- Deep Psychometric Guide & Theoretical Background -->
    <section class="max-w-4xl mx-auto w-full pt-10 border-t border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 space-y-8">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-bold text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          The Cognitive Neuroscience of Visual-Spatial Processing (Gv)
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          Pioneered by Roger Shepard and Jacqueline Metzler in their landmark 1971 mental rotation experiments, spatial cognition involves simulating physical object transformations in working memory. In the Cattell-Horn-Carroll taxonomy, Gv includes mental rotation (Vz), spatial relations (SR), and visualization (Vz).
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-3 gap-6">
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-2">
            1. 3D Isometric Cube Rotation
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
            Mentally spinning a 3-dimensional solid along the X, Y, or Z axes to determine whether an alternative angle represents an identical object or an impossible chiral reflection.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-2">
            2. Surface Folding Nets
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
            Folding a 2D cross or T-shaped flat net into a 6-sided 3D cube. Requires tracking which patterned faces end up adjacent versus on opposite sides.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-2">
            3. Orthographic Perspective
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
            Synthesizing multiple 2D views (top-down, front elevation, lateral profile) to reconstruct the correct composite 3D block geometry.
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>

      <!-- Strategy Tips for Test Takers -->
      <div class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-indigo-50/50 dark:bg-indigo-950/20 border border-indigo-100 dark:border-indigo-900/60">
        <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-indigo-950 dark:text-indigo-200 mb-3">
          Tactical Rules for Solving 3D Folding Net Puzzles
        </h3>
        <ul class="space-y-2.5 text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300">
          <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
            <span class="font-bold text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 shrink-0">&bull;</span>
            <span><strong>The Opposite Face Rule:</strong> In a standard 2D flat cube net, faces separated by exactly one square in a straight line will always be opposite each other when folded into a cube. Opposite faces can never be visible simultaneously from any vantage point.</span>
          </li>
          <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
            <span class="font-bold text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 shrink-0">&bull;</span>
            <span><strong>Anchor a Single Face:</strong> Pick the most distinct or asymmetrical face as your reference anchor, then fold adjacent faces relative to it.</span>
          </li>
          <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
            <span class="font-bold text-indigo-600 dark:text-indigo-400 shrink-0">&bull;</span>
            <span><strong>Verify Corner Triads:</strong> Check the 3 faces meeting at any visible vertex. Pay attention to directional pointers (arrows, diagonal lines) pointing toward or away from that shared corner.</span>
          </li>
        </ul>
      </div>

      <!-- Links to other batteries -->
      <div class="pt-6 border-t border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">

          Tactical Methods for Decoding Number Series

    <section class="max-w-4xl mx-auto w-full pt-10 border-t border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800 space-y-8">
      <div>
        <h2 class="font-heading text-2xl sm:text-3xl font-bold text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-3">
          The Foundations of Crystallized Intelligence &amp; Verbal Logic
        </h2>
        <p class="text-sm sm:text-base text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-300 leading-relaxed">
          While crystallized intelligence encompasses language and cultural knowledge, standardized psychometric tests of Gc focus on relational reasoning and formal deduction rather than superficial vocabulary recall. An individual must understand how abstract concepts connect, contrast, and establish necessary logical truths.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-3 gap-6">
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-2">
            1. Relational Analogies (A : B :: C : ?)
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
            Formulating the exact functional relationship between the primary word pair (part-to-whole, degree of intensity, causal agent, tool-to-action) and mapping it onto the target pair.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-2">
            2. Formal Deductive Syllogisms
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
            Evaluating arguments with two premise statements to determine which conclusion follows with absolute logical necessity, resisting the urge to rely on empirical belief bias.
          </p>
        </div>
        <div class="p-5 rounded-2xl bg-zinc-50 dark:bg-zinc-900 border border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
          <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-2">
            3. Categorical Inclusion Logic
          </h3>
          <p class="text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-600 dark:text-zinc-400 leading-relaxed">
            Classifying concepts into taxonomical hierarchies and identifying the unique term that violates a subtle underlying criterion (odd-one-out categorization).
          </p>
        </div>
      </div>

      <!-- Strategy Tips for Test Takers -->
      <div class="p-6 sm:p-8 rounded-3xl bg-purple-50/50 dark:bg-purple-950/20 border border-purple-100 dark:border-purple-900/60">
        <h3 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg text-purple-950 dark:text-purple-200 mb-3">
          Tactical Strategies for Formal Syllogisms &amp; Verbal Logic
        </h3>
        <ul class="space-y-2.5 text-xs sm:text-sm text-zinc-700 dark:text-zinc-300">
          <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
            <span class="font-bold text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 shrink-0">&bull;</span>
            <span><strong>Disregard Real-World Plausibility:</strong> Syllogisms must be solved strictly within the universe of the stated premises. Even if a premise contradicts empirical reality (e.g., "All apples are blue"), treat it as 100% true for the scope of the deduction.</span>
          </li>
          <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
            <span class="font-bold text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 shrink-0">&bull;</span>
            <span><strong>Formulate an Exact Bridging Sentence for Analogies:</strong> In analogies (e.g., Thermometer : Temperature :: Barometer : ?), build a precise sentence: "A thermometer is an instrument designed to measure temperature; therefore, a barometer is an instrument designed to measure atmospheric pressure."</span>
          </li>
          <li class="flex items-start gap-2">
            <span class="font-bold text-purple-600 dark:text-purple-400 shrink-0">&bull;</span>
            <span><strong>Beware of the "Some" Trap:</strong> In formal logic, "Some" means "At least one, and possibly all." It does NOT imply "Some are not." Diagram overlapping sets or Venn circles mentally to prevent invalid quantifier leaps.</span>
          </li>
        </ul>
      </div>

      <!-- Links to other batteries -->
      <div class="pt-6 border-t border-zinc-200 dark:border-zinc-800">
        <h3 class="font-heading font-semibold text-base text-zinc-900 dark:text-white mb-4">
          Explore Other Specialized Cognitive Batteries
        </h3>
        <div class="grid grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-3 gap-4">

  const form = document.getElementById('contact-form') as HTMLFormElement;
  const feedback = document.getElementById('form-feedback');
  if (form && feedback) {
    form.addEventListener('submit', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();
      feedback.classList.remove('hidden');
      form.reset();
    });
  }
</script>

          1. Client-Side Evaluation Architecture
        </h2>
        <p>
          At FreeIQExam.com, we respect your fundamental right to digital privacy. Unlike conventional assessment platforms that store responses on central servers, <strong>all scoring algorithms, 2PL IRT estimations, and percentile conversions execute 100% locally in your web browser runtime</strong>.
        </p>
        <p>
          Your individual item answers, elapsed time, and calculated IQ estimates are never transmitted to our web servers, database repositories, or commercial advertising networks.
        </p>
      </section>

      <section>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg sm:text-xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          2. Local Storage Usage
        </h2>
        <p>
          We utilize standard HTML5 local storage (localStorage) exclusively to provide functional convenience during your assessment session:
        </p>
        <ul class="list-disc pl-5 space-y-1">
          <li><strong>freeiq_active_session:</strong> Temporarily stores your answers and remaining timer seconds so your progress is preserved if you accidentally refresh the page.</li>
          <li><strong>freeiq_test_answers:</strong> Stores your finalized responses upon submission so the results page can compute your score.</li>
          <li><strong>theme:</strong> Remembers your light or dark mode aesthetic preference.</li>
        </ul>
        <p class="mt-2">
          You can erase this data at any time simply by clearing your browser's site data or storage, or by clicking "Retake Test".
        </p>
      </section>

      <section>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg sm:text-xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          3. No Collection of Personally Identifiable Information (PII)
        </h2>
        <p>
          We do not require, collect, or store your name, email address, postal address, phone number, social media handles, or payment credentials. You may complete the test without providing personal identification.
        </p>
      </section>

      <section>
        <h2 class="font-heading font-bold text-lg sm:text-xl text-zinc-950 dark:text-white mb-2">
          4. Zero Sale of Personal Data
        </h2>
        <p>
          Under the California Consumer Privacy Act (CCPA) and European Union General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), we affirm that FreeIQExam does not sell, rent, license, or barter user data to data brokers or third-party marketers.
        </p>
      </section>
